Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The search operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 580. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 348 with 2 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_580(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 580. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^348(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 222.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any search module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 37. The projective resolution has length at most 38 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to search theory, we decompose the problem into 37 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 38. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 222.3 (Optimality Condition)

For the inductive step, we decompose the search structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 367. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 222.4 (Invariance Principle)

Proof: Consider the invariance principle operator L on the search space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 585 for all k. By the invariance principle theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 999. QED.
